- **linux-hardening**: Assess and harden Linux servers against CIS Benchmarks — SSH configuration, accounts and sudo, patching, filesystem mount options, kernel sysctls, service minimization, MAC (SELinux/AppArmor), logging — using Lynis, OpenSCAP and manual checks. Use when building a server baseline, reviewing a hardening report, or remediating Linux findings. ([kaandgnsh/Claude-Blue](https://github.com/kaandgnsh/Claude-Blue/tree/HEAD/skills/linux-security/linux-hardening))
- **linux-hardening**: Use when hardening a Linux server for production, when locking down SSH with key-only authentication, when writing a default-deny firewall with ufw or nftables, when disabling unneeded services, when enabling automatic security updates, when adding auditd rules for sensitive files, and when running a CIS-style checklist adapted for small teams. ([mizazhaider-ceh/AgentArsenal](https://github.com/mizazhaider-ceh/AgentArsenal/tree/HEAD/skills/linux/linux-hardening))
- **linux-hardening-baseline**: Use when building a defensive Linux hardening baseline for a server fleet, audit, or compliance review. Covers the baseline workflow (inventory, benchmark selection, apply, verify, monitor), SSH hardening with OpenSSH, filesystem permission hygiene, kernel parameter tuning with sysctl, and mapping controls to CIS benchmarks and MITRE ATT&CK mitigations. Purely defensive, with no exploitation content, covering how to make systems harder to break into and easier to audit. ([mizazhaider-ceh/AgentArsenal](https://github.com/mizazhaider-ceh/AgentArsenal/tree/HEAD/skills/linux/linux-hardening-baseline))
- **linux-hardening-baseline-filesystem-permissions**: Use when auditing or hardening Linux filesystem permissions as part of a defensive baseline. Covers correct ownership and modes for /etc/passwd, /etc/shadow, /etc/ssh and cron paths, finding unexpected SUID and SGID binaries, hunting world-writable files, sticky bit verification on /tmp, umask policy, and ACL auditing. Defensive hardening mapped to MITRE ATT&CK mitigations for T1222 File and Directory Permissions Modification and T1548 Abuse Elevation Control Mechanism. ([mizazhaider-ceh/AgentArsenal](https://github.com/mizazhaider-ceh/AgentArsenal/tree/HEAD/skills/linux/linux-hardening-baseline/sub/linux-hardening-baseline-filesystem-permissions))
- **linux-hardening-baseline-kernel-parameters**: Use when tuning Linux kernel parameters with sysctl as part of a defensive hardening baseline. Covers exploit mitigations (ASLR, kptr_restrict, dmesg_restrict, core dumps, module loading), network stack hardening (SYN cookies, IP spoofing protection, redirects, source routing), persisting settings in /etc/sysctl.d, verifying after reboot, and documenting deviations. Defensive hardening mapped to MITRE ATT&CK mitigations for T1068 Exploitation for Privilege Escalation. ([mizazhaider-ceh/AgentArsenal](https://github.com/mizazhaider-ceh/AgentArsenal/tree/HEAD/skills/linux/linux-hardening-baseline/sub/linux-hardening-baseline-kernel-parameters))
- **linux-hardening-baseline-ssh-hardening**: Use when hardening OpenSSH on a Linux server or auditing an existing sshd configuration. Covers key-only authentication, disabling root login and password auth, AllowUsers and AllowGroups scoping, strong ciphers MACs and key exchange selection, idle timeouts and login attempt limits, verifying effective config with sshd -T, and brute force defenses with fail2ban. Defensive hardening mapped to MITRE ATT&CK mitigations for T1110 Brute Force and T1078 Valid Accounts. ([mizazhaider-ceh/AgentArsenal](https://github.com/mizazhaider-ceh/AgentArsenal/tree/HEAD/skills/linux/linux-hardening-baseline/sub/linux-hardening-baseline-ssh-hardening))
- **linux-hardware-troubleshooting**: Diagnose and fix Linux hardware issues — audio (ALSA/PipeWire/PulseAudio, HDA codec pins, modprobe model overrides), WiFi (regulatory domains, ath12k/ath11k/iwlwifi driver quirks, self-managed firmware), GPU/X server (hybrid GPU config conflicts, Xorg fatal errors, kwin crashes, NVIDIA driver interactions). Covers diagnostic workflows, modprobe parameter fixes, firmware quirks, and hardware-specific workarounds. Trigger: user reports audio, WiFi, or system freeze/hang symptoms on Linux. ([ShiganLuo/skill](https://github.com/ShiganLuo/skill/tree/HEAD/linux-hardware-troubleshooting))
